# FILE: observability/prometheus/rules/alerts.rules.yml   (133 lines)
####################################################################################################
  1 | groups:
  2 |     #agrupamos las reglas de alerta en un grupo llamado "manage-system"
  3 |   - name: infrastructure.rules
  4 |     rules:
  5 |         #declaramos una alerta llamada HighCPUUsage que se activara cuando el uso de CPU supere el 85% durante 5 minutos.
  6 |       - alert: HighCPUUsage
  7 |         expr: 100 - (avg by(instance) (rate(node_cpu_seconds_total{mode="idle"}[5m])) * 100) > 85
  8 |         for: 5m
  9 |         labels:
 10 |           severity: warning
 11 |           team: infra
 12 |           alert_group: cpu
 13 |         annotations:
 14 |           summary: "CPU alta en {{ $labels.instance }}"
 15 |           description: "El uso de CPU en {{ $labels.instance }} lleva más de 5m por encima del 85% (actual: {{ $value | printf \"%.1f\" }}%)."
 16 | 
 17 |         #declaramos una alerta llamada CriticalCPUUsage que se activara cuando el uso de CPU supere el 95% durante 3 minutos.
 18 |       - alert: CriticalCPUUsage
 19 |         expr: 100 - (avg by(instance) (rate(node_cpu_seconds_total{mode="idle"}[5m])) * 100) > 95
 20 |         for: 3m
 21 |         labels:
 22 |           severity: critical
 23 |           team: infra
 24 |           alert_group: cpu
 25 |         annotations:
 26 |           summary: "CPU crítica en {{ $labels.instance }}"
 27 |           description: "El uso de CPU en {{ $labels.instance }} supera el 95% durante más de 3m (actual: {{ $value | printf \"%.1f\" }}%)."
 28 | 
 29 |         #declaramos una alerta llamada HighMemoryUsage que se activara cuando el uso de memoria supere el 90% durante 5 minutos.
 30 |       - alert: HighMemoryUsage
 31 |         expr: (1 - (node_memory_MemAvailable_bytes / node_memory_MemTotal_bytes)) * 100 > 90
 32 |         for: 5m
 33 |         labels:
 34 |           severity: warning
 35 |           team: infra
 36 |         annotations:
 37 |           summary: "Memoria alta en {{ $labels.instance }}"
 38 |           description: "Uso de memoria en {{ $labels.instance }} por encima del 90% durante 5m (actual: {{ $value | printf \"%.1f\" }}%)."
 39 | 
 40 |         #declaramos una alerta llamada CriticalMemoryUsage que se activara cuando el uso de memoria supere el 95% durante 3 minutos.
 41 |       - alert: DBPoolNearExhaustion
 42 |         expr: (db_pool_active_connections / db_pool_max_connections) * 100 > 90
 43 |         for: 3m
 44 |         labels:
 45 |           severity: critical
 46 |           team: infra
 47 |         annotations:
 48 |           summary: "Pool de conexiones de DB casi agotado"
 49 |           description: "El pool de conexiones está al {{ $value | printf \"%.1f\" }}% de su capacidad durante más de 3m."
 50 | 
 51 | 
 52 |   - name: application.rules
 53 |     rules:
 54 |         #declaramos una alerta llamada HighErrorRate que se activara cuando la tasa de errores HTTP 5xx en Flask supere el 5% durante 5 minutos.
 55 |       - alert: HighErrorRate
 56 |         expr: (sum(rate(flask_http_request_total{status=~"5.."}[5m])) / sum(rate(flask_http_request_total[5m]))) * 100 > 5
 57 |         for: 5m
 58 |         labels:
 59 |           severity: critical
 60 |           team: backend
 61 |           alert_group: error_rate
 62 |         annotations:
 63 |           summary: "Tasa de error alta"
 64 |           description: "La tasa de errores 5xx lleva más de 5m por encima del 5% (actual: {{ $value | printf \"%.2f\" }}%)."
 65 | 
 66 |         #declaramos una alerta llamada ElevatedErrorRate que se activara cuando la tasa de errores HTTP 5xx en Flask supere el 1% durante 10 minutos.
 67 |       - alert: ElevatedErrorRate
 68 |         expr: (sum(rate(flask_http_request_total{status=~"5.."}[5m])) / sum(rate(flask_http_request_total[5m]))) * 100 > 1
 69 |         for: 10m
 70 |         labels:
 71 |           severity: warning
 72 |           team: backend
 73 |           alert_group: error_rate
 74 |         annotations:
 75 |           summary: "Tasa de error elevada"
 76 |           description: "La tasa de errores 5xx supera el 1% durante más de 10m (actual: {{ $value | printf \"%.2f\" }}%)."
 77 | 
 78 |         #declaramos una alerta llamada HighLatencyP95 que se activara cuando la latencia p95 de las solicitudes HTTP en Flask supere 1 segundo durante 5 minutos.
 79 |       - alert: HighLatencyP95
 80 |         expr: histogram_quantile(0.95, sum(rate(flask_http_request_duration_seconds_bucket[5m])) by (le)) > 1
 81 |         for: 5m
 82 |         labels:
 83 |           severity: warning
 84 |           team: backend
 85 |         annotations:
 86 |           summary: "Latencia p95 alta"
 87 |           description: "La latencia p95 supera 1s durante más de 5m (actual: {{ $value | printf \"%.2f\" }}s)."
 88 | 
 89 |   - name: security.rules
 90 |     rules:
 91 |         #declaramos una alerta llamada HighAuthFailureRate que se activara cuando la tasa de fallos de autenticación supere 1 fallo/s durante 5 minutos.
 92 |       - alert: HighAuthFailureRate
 93 |         expr: sum(rate(auth_failures_total[5m])) > 1
 94 |         for: 5m
 95 |         labels:
 96 |           severity: critical
 97 |           team: security
 98 |         annotations:
 99 |           summary: "Tasa alta de fallos de autenticación"
100 |           description: "Se detectan más de 1 fallo/s de autenticación durante 5m (actual: {{ $value | printf \"%.2f\" }}/s). Posible ataque de fuerza bruta."
101 | 
102 |         #declaramos una alerta llamada SpikeInvalidTokens que se activara cuando el número de tokens inválidos aumente en más de 50 en 15 minutos.
103 |       - alert: SpikeInvalidTokens
104 |         expr: increase(invalid_tokens_total[15m]) > 50
105 |         for: 0m
106 |         labels:
107 |           severity: warning
108 |           team: security
109 |         annotations:
110 |           summary: "Pico de tokens inválidos"
111 |           description: "Se registraron {{ $value }} tokens inválidos en los últimos 15 minutos."
112 | 
113 |         #declaramos una alerta llamada AuthFailuresFromSingleIP que se activara cuando una misma IP genere más de 20 fallos de autenticación en 10 minutos.
114 |       - alert: AuthFailuresFromSingleIP
115 |         expr: sum by(source_ip) (increase(auth_failures_total[10m])) > 20
116 |         for: 0m
117 |         labels:
118 |           severity: critical
119 |           team: security
120 |         annotations:
121 |           summary: "Múltiples fallos de auth desde una misma IP"
122 |           description: "La IP {{ $labels.source_ip }} generó {{ $value }} fallos de autenticación en 10 minutos."
123 | 
124 |         #declaramos una alerta llamada KeycloakFailedLogins que se activara cuando una misma IP falle mas de 5 logins en Keycloak en 5 minutos (posible fuerza bruta). La metrica sale de los logs de Keycloak via Alloy.
125 |       - alert: KeycloakFailedLogins
126 |         expr: sum by(source_ip) (increase(loki_process_custom_keycloak_login_failures_total[5m])) > 5
127 |         for: 0m
128 |         labels:
129 |           severity: critical
130 |           team: security
131 |         annotations:
132 |           summary: "Muchos logins fallidos en Keycloak"
133 |           description: "La IP {{ $labels.source_ip }} falló {{ $value | printf \"%.0f\" }} logins en los últimos 5 minutos."

 3 |   - name: manage-system
 4 |     rules:
 5 |         # (la alerta HighErrorRate vive solo en alerts.rules.yml; tenerla duplicada con otra expresion causaba conflicto)

 9 |         for: 1m

####################################################################################################
# FILE: scripts/ci-free-host-ports.sh   (37 lines)
####################################################################################################
 1 | #!/usr/bin/env bash
 2 | # Stop containers blocking CI host ports (leftover dev stacks, failed builds).
 3 | set -euo pipefail
 4 | 
 5 | COMPOSE_CI="${COMPOSE_CI:-docker-compose.ci.yml}"
 6 | PORTS="${CI_PORTS:-5432 8080 5000}"
 7 | KEEP_PROJECTS="${CI_KEEP_PROJECTS:-}"
 8 | 
 9 | keep_project() {
10 |   local proj="$1"
11 |   [[ -z "$KEEP_PROJECTS" ]] && return 1
12 |   local keep
13 |   for keep in $KEEP_PROJECTS; do
14 |     [[ "$proj" == "$keep" || "$proj" == "${keep}-data" || "$proj" == "${keep}-cov" ]] && return 0
15 |   done
16 |   return 1
17 | }
18 | 
19 | for net in $(docker network ls --format '{{.Name}}' | grep -E '^manage-ci-[0-9]+(-[a-z]+)?_default$' || true); do
20 |   proj="${net%_default}"
21 |   if keep_project "$proj"; then
22 |     continue
23 |   fi
24 |   echo "==> Removing stale compose project: $proj"
25 |   docker compose -f "$COMPOSE_CI" -p "$proj" down -v --remove-orphans 2>/dev/null || true
26 | done
27 | 
28 | for port in $PORTS; do
29 |   while read -r cid proj; do
30 |     [[ -z "$cid" ]] && continue
31 |     if keep_project "$proj"; then
32 |       continue
33 |     fi
34 |     echo "==> Stopping container $cid (project=${proj:-unknown}) on port $port"
35 |     docker stop "$cid" >/dev/null 2>&1 || true
36 |   done < <(docker ps --filter "publish=$port" --format '{{.ID}} {{.Label "com.docker.compose.project"}}' 2>/dev/null || true)
37 | done

####################################################################################################
# FILE: scripts/ci-http-check.sh   (41 lines)
####################################################################################################
 1 | #!/usr/bin/env bash
 2 | # HTTP helper for CI (Jenkins-in-Docker reaches host services via host.docker.internal).
 3 | ci_resolve_url() {
 4 |   local url="$1"
 5 |   if [[ "${CI_WAIT_USE_HOST_NETWORK:-}" == "1" ]]; then
 6 |     echo "${url//localhost/host.docker.internal}"
 7 |   else
 8 |     echo "$url"
 9 |   fi
10 | }
11 | 
12 | ci_curl() {
13 |   local args=("$@")
14 |   local resolved=()
15 |   local needs_host_header=false
16 | 
17 |   for arg in "${args[@]}"; do
18 |     local next
19 |     next="$(ci_resolve_url "$arg")"
20 |     resolved+=("$next")
21 |     if [[ "$next" == *":8080"* ]]; then
22 |       needs_host_header=true
23 |     fi
24 |   done
25 | 
26 |   if [[ "${CI_WAIT_USE_HOST_NETWORK:-}" == "1" && "$needs_host_header" == "true" ]]; then
27 |     curl -H "Host: localhost" "${resolved[@]}"
28 |   else
29 |     curl "${resolved[@]}"
30 |   fi
31 | }
32 | 
33 | ci_http_ok() {
34 |   local url
35 |   url="$(ci_resolve_url "$1")"
36 |   if [[ "${CI_WAIT_USE_HOST_NETWORK:-}" == "1" && "$url" == *":8080"* ]]; then
37 |     curl -fsSL -H "Host: localhost" "$url" >/dev/null 2>&1
38 |   else
39 |     curl -fsSL "$url" >/dev/null 2>&1
40 |   fi
41 | }

####################################################################################################
# FILE: scripts/ci-verify-e2e-ready.sh   (24 lines)
####################################################################################################
 1 | #!/usr/bin/env bash
 2 | # Verify CI services from inside the compose network (avoids Jenkins localhost:8080 clash).
 3 | set -euo pipefail
 4 | 
 5 | COMPOSE_FILE="${COMPOSE_FILE:-docker-compose.ci.yml}"
 6 | COMPOSE_PROJECT="${COMPOSE_PROJECT:?COMPOSE_PROJECT is required}"
 7 | 
 8 | if [[ -n "${DOCKER_COMPOSE:-}" ]]; then
 9 |   compose() { "$DOCKER_COMPOSE" -f "$COMPOSE_FILE" -p "$COMPOSE_PROJECT" "$@"; }
10 | elif command -v docker-compose >/dev/null 2>&1; then
11 |   compose() { docker-compose -f "$COMPOSE_FILE" -p "$COMPOSE_PROJECT" "$@"; }
12 | else
13 |   compose() { docker compose -f "$COMPOSE_FILE" -p "$COMPOSE_PROJECT" "$@"; }
14 | fi
15 | 
16 | echo "==> Checking Keycloak realm via compose network"
17 | compose exec -T web python scripts/wait-for-keycloak-realm.py
18 | 
19 | echo "==> Checking Flask login page via compose network"
20 | compose exec -T web python -c "
21 | import urllib.request
22 | urllib.request.urlopen('http://127.0.0.1:5000/auth/login-page', timeout=5)
23 | print('OK  Flask login page')
24 | "

####################################################################################################
# FILE: scripts/cloudflare-tunnel.sh   (319 lines)
####################################################################################################
  1 | #!/usr/bin/env bash
  2 | # Expose Flask + Keycloak on the public internet via Cloudflare quick tunnels
  3 | # (trycloudflare.com) so a demo can be reached without VPN or your own domain.
  4 | #
  5 | # Usage:
  6 | #   bash scripts/cloudflare-tunnel.sh          # start tunnels + reconfigure OAuth
  7 | #   bash scripts/cloudflare-tunnel.sh stop     # tear down tunnels + restore localhost
  8 | #   bash scripts/cloudflare-tunnel.sh status   # show URLs if running
  9 | #
 10 | # Requirements: Docker stack up (web + keycloak; also grafana if you want it
 11 | # tunneled — it's optional and tunneled automatically if it's already running).
 12 | # curl, python3 required.
 13 | # cloudflared is downloaded to .tools/ if missing (no Cloudflare account needed).
 14 | #
 15 | # localhost:5000 / localhost:8080 keep working the entire time this is running,
 16 | # and are the ONLY thing that works again once you run "stop" — nothing here
 17 | # permanently repoints the app away from localhost.
 18 | set -euo pipefail
 19 | 
 20 | ROOT_DIR="$(cd "$(dirname "${BASH_SOURCE[0]}")/.." && pwd)"
 21 | cd "$ROOT_DIR"
 22 | 
 23 | STATE_DIR="${ROOT_DIR}/.cloudflare-tunnel"
 24 | TOOLS_DIR="${ROOT_DIR}/.tools"
 25 | ENV_FILE="${ROOT_DIR}/.env.cloudflare"
 26 | CLOUDFLARED="${CLOUDFLARED:-${TOOLS_DIR}/cloudflared}"
 27 | COMPOSE="${DOCKER_COMPOSE:-docker compose}"
 28 | 
 29 | mkdir -p "$STATE_DIR" "$TOOLS_DIR"
 30 | 
 31 | log() { echo "==> $*"; }
 32 | die() { echo "ERROR: $*" >&2; exit 1; }
 33 | 
 34 | ensure_cloudflared() {
 35 |   if command -v cloudflared >/dev/null 2>&1; then
 36 |     CLOUDFLARED="$(command -v cloudflared)"
 37 |     return
 38 |   fi
 39 |   if [[ -x "$CLOUDFLARED" ]]; then
 40 |     return
 41 |   fi
 42 |   log "Downloading cloudflared to ${CLOUDFLARED}..."
 43 |   local url arch
 44 |   arch="$(uname -m)"
 45 |   case "$arch" in
 46 |     x86_64|amd64) url="https://github.com/cloudflare/cloudflared/releases/latest/download/cloudflared-linux-amd64" ;;
 47 |     aarch64|arm64) url="https://github.com/cloudflare/cloudflared/releases/latest/download/cloudflared-linux-arm64" ;;
 48 |     *) die "Unsupported architecture: $arch (install cloudflared manually)" ;;
 49 |   esac
 50 |   curl -fsSL "$url" -o "$CLOUDFLARED"
 51 |   chmod +x "$CLOUDFLARED"
 52 | }
 53 | 
 54 | wait_local() {
 55 |   local name="$1" url="$2" attempts="${3:-60}"
 56 |   log "Waiting for ${name} (${url})..."
 57 |   local i
 58 |   for i in $(seq 1 "$attempts"); do
 59 |     if curl -sf -o /dev/null "$url"; then
 60 |       echo "OK  ${name}"
 61 |       return 0
 62 |     fi
 63 |     sleep 2
 64 |   done
 65 |   die "${name} not reachable at ${url}. Start the stack first: docker compose up -d web keycloak"
 66 | }
 67 | 
 68 | start_one_tunnel() {
 69 |   local name="$1" target="$2"
 70 |   local log_file="${STATE_DIR}/${name}.log"
 71 |   local pid_file="${STATE_DIR}/${name}.pid"
 72 |   local url_file="${STATE_DIR}/${name}.url"
 73 | 
 74 |   if [[ -f "$pid_file" ]] && kill -0 "$(cat "$pid_file")" 2>/dev/null; then
 75 |     log "Tunnel ${name} already running (pid $(cat "$pid_file"))"
 76 |     return 0
 77 |   fi
 78 | 
 79 |   : >"$log_file"
 80 |   "$CLOUDFLARED" tunnel --no-autoupdate --url "$target" >"$log_file" 2>&1 &
 81 |   echo $! >"$pid_file"
 82 | 
 83 |   local i url=""
 84 |   for i in $(seq 1 45); do
 85 |     url="$(grep -Eo 'https://[a-zA-Z0-9.-]+\.trycloudflare\.com' "$log_file" | head -1 || true)"
 86 |     if [[ -n "$url" ]]; then
 87 |       echo "$url" >"$url_file"
 88 |       log "${name} public URL: ${url}"
 89 |       return 0
 90 |     fi
 91 |     if ! kill -0 "$(cat "$pid_file")" 2>/dev/null; then
 92 |       tail -n 40 "$log_file" >&2 || true
 93 |       die "cloudflared for ${name} exited early"
 94 |     fi
 95 |     sleep 1
 96 |   done
 97 |   tail -n 40 "$log_file" >&2 || true
 98 |   die "Timed out waiting for ${name} trycloudflare.com URL"
 99 | }
100 | 
101 | stop_tunnels() {
102 |   local name pid_file
103 |   for name in flask keycloak grafana; do
104 |     pid_file="${STATE_DIR}/${name}.pid"
105 |     if [[ -f "$pid_file" ]]; then
106 |       local pid
107 |       pid="$(cat "$pid_file")"
108 |       if kill -0 "$pid" 2>/dev/null; then
109 |         log "Stopping ${name} tunnel (pid ${pid})"
110 |         kill "$pid" 2>/dev/null || true
111 |         wait "$pid" 2>/dev/null || true
112 |       fi
113 |       rm -f "$pid_file"
114 |     fi
115 |   done
116 | }
117 | 
118 | # Update the Keycloak "flask-backend" client so BOTH the given external URLs
119 | # AND localhost keep being valid callback/logout targets at the same time.
120 | # This is what lets localhost:5000 keep working while a tunnel is active, and
121 | # is exactly what's left behind (localhost-only) once the tunnel is stopped.
122 | configure_keycloak_client() {
123 |   local flask_url="$1"
124 |   local kc_url="$2"
125 |   local callback="${flask_url%/}/auth/callback"
126 | 
127 |   log "Updating Keycloak client flask-backend redirect URIs -> ${callback} (+ localhost)"
128 | 
129 |   $COMPOSE exec -T \
130 |     -e KC_ADMIN="${KEYCLOAK_USER:-admin}" \
131 |     -e KC_ADMIN_PASSWORD="${KEYCLOAK_PASSWORD:-admin}" \
132 |     -e CALLBACK="$callback" \
133 |     -e FLASK_URL="$flask_url" \
134 |     keycloak bash -ec '
135 |     /opt/keycloak/bin/kcadm.sh config credentials \
136 |       --server http://localhost:8080 \
137 |       --realm master \
138 |       --user "$KC_ADMIN" \
139 |       --password "$KC_ADMIN_PASSWORD"
140 |     CID=$(/opt/keycloak/bin/kcadm.sh get clients -r inventory-realm -q clientId=flask-backend --fields id --format csv --noquotes | tail -1)
141 |     test -n "$CID"
142 | 
143 |     # Allow both http and https callback variants (ProxyFix / scheme quirks).
144 |     HTTPS_CB="${CALLBACK/http:/https:}"
145 |     HTTP_CB="${CALLBACK/https:/http:}"
146 |     HTTPS_ROOT="${FLASK_URL/http:/https:}"
147 |     HTTP_ROOT="${FLASK_URL/https:/http:}"
148 | 
149 |     # Keycloak post-logout URIs are "##"-separated; keep both the public URL
150 |     # and localhost valid at all times so neither ever breaks the other.
151 |     POST_LOGOUT="${HTTPS_ROOT%/}/*##${HTTPS_ROOT%/}/##${HTTP_ROOT%/}/*##${HTTP_ROOT%/}/##http://localhost:5000/*##http://localhost:5000/"
152 | 
153 |     /opt/keycloak/bin/kcadm.sh update "clients/$CID" -r inventory-realm \
154 |       -s "redirectUris=[\"$HTTP_CB\",\"$HTTPS_CB\",\"http://localhost:5000/auth/callback\"]" \
155 |       -s "webOrigins=[\"$FLASK_URL\",\"http://localhost:5000\",\"*\"]" \
156 |       -s "attributes.\"post.logout.redirect.uris\"=$POST_LOGOUT"
157 |   '
158 | }
159 | 
160 | write_env_cloudflare() {
161 |   local flask_url="$1"
162 |   local kc_url="$2"
163 |   cat >"$ENV_FILE" <<EOF
164 | # Generated by scripts/cloudflare-tunnel.sh — do not commit
165 | FLASK_PUBLIC_URL=${flask_url}
166 | KEYCLOAK_PUBLIC_URL=${kc_url}
167 | KC_HOSTNAME_URL=${kc_url}
168 | KC_HOSTNAME_ADMIN_URL=${kc_url}
169 | KC_PROXY_HEADERS=xforwarded
170 | KEYCLOAK_ISSUER=${kc_url}/realms/inventory-realm
171 | KEYCLOAK_AUTHORIZE_URL=${kc_url}/realms/inventory-realm/protocol/openid-connect/auth
172 | KEYCLOAK_LOGOUT_BASE=${kc_url}/realms/inventory-realm/protocol/openid-connect/logout
173 | EOF
174 |   log "Wrote ${ENV_FILE}"
175 | }
176 | 
177 | apply_public_config() {
178 |   local flask_url="$1"
179 |   local kc_url="$2"
180 |   write_env_cloudflare "$flask_url" "$kc_url"
181 | 
182 |   log "Applying public hostnames to Keycloak + Flask (restart, no rebuild)..."
183 |   set -a
184 |   # shellcheck disable=SC1090
185 |   source "$ENV_FILE"
186 |   set +a
187 |   # Recreate only if config actually changed (no --force-recreate): on small
188 |   # machines, unconditionally recreating every container here has previously
189 |   # caused the app to crash under memory pressure and serve 502s.
190 |   $COMPOSE --env-file .env --env-file "$ENV_FILE" up -d --no-deps keycloak web
191 |   $COMPOSE --env-file .env --env-file "$ENV_FILE" restart keycloak web
192 | 
193 |   wait_local "Keycloak (local port)" "http://localhost:8080/realms/inventory-realm/.well-known/openid-configuration" 90
194 |   wait_local "Flask (local port)" "http://localhost:5000/auth/login-page" 60
195 | 
196 |   sleep 3
197 |   configure_keycloak_client "$flask_url" "$kc_url"
198 | 
199 |   if [[ -f scripts/prepare-keycloak-e2e.sh ]]; then
200 |     log "Ensuring demo users (password123)..."
201 |     COMPOSE_FILE=docker-compose.yml bash scripts/prepare-keycloak-e2e.sh || true
202 |   fi
203 | }
204 | 
205 | restore_localhost() {
206 |   log "Restoring localhost-only Keycloak / Flask configuration..."
207 |   rm -f "$ENV_FILE"
208 |   unset KC_HOSTNAME_URL KC_HOSTNAME_ADMIN_URL KC_PROXY_HEADERS KEYCLOAK_ISSUER \
209 |     KEYCLOAK_AUTHORIZE_URL KEYCLOAK_LOGOUT_BASE FLASK_PUBLIC_URL KEYCLOAK_PUBLIC_URL || true
210 | 
211 |   $COMPOSE --env-file .env up -d --no-deps keycloak web
212 |   $COMPOSE --env-file .env restart keycloak web
213 | 
214 |   wait_local "Keycloak (local port)" "http://localhost:8080/realms/inventory-realm/.well-known/openid-configuration" 90
215 |   wait_local "Flask (local port)" "http://localhost:5000/auth/login-page" 60
216 | 
217 |   if [[ -f .env ]]; then set -a; source .env; set +a; fi
218 |   configure_keycloak_client "http://localhost:5000" "http://localhost:8080" || true
219 |   log "Restored. Local app: http://localhost:5000 (fully independent of the tunnel now)"
220 | }
221 | 
222 | print_status() {
223 |   local flask_url kc_url grafana_url
224 |   flask_url="$(cat "${STATE_DIR}/flask.url" 2>/dev/null || true)"
225 |   kc_url="$(cat "${STATE_DIR}/keycloak.url" 2>/dev/null || true)"
226 |   grafana_url="$(cat "${STATE_DIR}/grafana.url" 2>/dev/null || true)"
227 |   echo ""
228 |   echo "========== Cloudflare tunnel status =========="
229 |   if [[ -n "$flask_url" ]]; then
230 |     echo "  App (share this):     ${flask_url}"
231 |     echo "  Login page:           ${flask_url}/auth/login-page"
232 |   else
233 |     echo "  App tunnel:           (not running)"
234 |   fi
235 |   if [[ -n "$kc_url" ]]; then
236 |     echo "  Keycloak (public):    ${kc_url}"
237 |   else
238 |     echo "  Keycloak tunnel:      (not running)"
239 |   fi
240 |   if [[ -n "$grafana_url" ]]; then
241 |     echo "  Grafana (public):     ${grafana_url}"
242 |     echo "                        login required -> \${GRAFANA_ADMIN_USER:-admin} / \${GRAFANA_ADMIN_PASSWORD:-admin} (see .env)"
243 |   else
244 |     echo "  Grafana tunnel:       (not running — start 'grafana' in Docker first if you want it)"
245 |   fi
246 |   echo ""
247 |   echo "  Local app (always works, tunnel or not): http://localhost:5000"
248 |   echo ""
249 |   echo "  Demo login: kratos_boss / password123  (manager)"
250 |   echo "              alice_worker / password123 (employee)"
251 |   echo ""
252 |   echo "  Stop:  bash scripts/cloudflare-tunnel.sh stop"
253 |   echo "=============================================="
254 | }
255 | 
256 | cmd_start() {
257 |   if [[ -f .env ]]; then
258 |     set -a
259 |     # shellcheck disable=SC1091
260 |     source .env
261 |     set +a
262 |   fi
263 |   ensure_cloudflared
264 |   wait_local "Flask" "http://localhost:5000/auth/login-page" 30
265 |   wait_local "Keycloak" "http://localhost:8080/realms/inventory-realm/.well-known/openid-configuration" 30
266 | 
267 |   log "Starting Cloudflare quick tunnels (no account required)..."
268 |   start_one_tunnel flask "http://127.0.0.1:5000"
269 |   start_one_tunnel keycloak "http://127.0.0.1:8080"
270 | 
271 |   # Grafana is optional and not part of the OAuth setup — only tunnel it if it's
272 |   # already up (docker compose up -d grafana). Never blocks the app/Keycloak tunnels.
273 |   if curl -sf -o /dev/null "http://localhost:3000/api/health" 2>/dev/null; then
274 |     start_one_tunnel grafana "http://127.0.0.1:3000"
275 |   else
276 |     log "Grafana not running locally on :3000 — skipping Grafana tunnel (this is fine)."
277 |   fi
278 | 
279 |   local flask_url kc_url
280 |   flask_url="$(cat "${STATE_DIR}/flask.url")"
281 |   kc_url="$(cat "${STATE_DIR}/keycloak.url")"
282 | 
283 |   apply_public_config "$flask_url" "$kc_url"
284 |   print_status
285 | 
286 |   log "Tunnels keep running in the background."
287 |   log "PIDs: flask=$(cat "${STATE_DIR}/flask.pid") keycloak=$(cat "${STATE_DIR}/keycloak.pid")$( [[ -f "${STATE_DIR}/grafana.pid" ]] && echo " grafana=$(cat "${STATE_DIR}/grafana.pid")" )"
288 |   log "localhost:5000 and localhost:8080 both still work normally while this is running."
289 | }
290 | 
291 | cmd_stop() {
292 |   stop_tunnels
293 |   restore_localhost
294 |   rm -f "${STATE_DIR}"/{flask,keycloak,grafana}.url "${STATE_DIR}"/{flask,keycloak,grafana}.log
295 |   log "Cloudflare tunnels stopped. Only localhost works now — exactly as before this script ran."
296 | }
297 | 
298 | cmd_status() {
299 |   print_status
300 |   local name pid_file
301 |   for name in flask keycloak grafana; do
302 |     pid_file="${STATE_DIR}/${name}.pid"
303 |     if [[ -f "$pid_file" ]] && kill -0 "$(cat "$pid_file")" 2>/dev/null; then
304 |       echo "  ${name} tunnel pid: $(cat "$pid_file") (running)"
305 |     else
306 |       echo "  ${name} tunnel: not running"
307 |     fi
308 |   done
309 | }
310 | 
311 | case "${1:-start}" in
312 |   start) cmd_start ;;
313 |   stop) cmd_stop ;;
314 |   status) cmd_status ;;
315 |   *)
316 |     echo "Usage: $0 [start|stop|status]"
317 |     exit 1
318 |     ;;
319 | esac

####################################################################################################
# FILE: scripts/generate-code-reference.py   (845 lines)
####################################################################################################
  1 | #!/usr/bin/env python3
  2 | """Build docs/FULL_CODE_REFERENCE.txt: a plain-text reference of the whole project.
  3 | 
  4 | Hand-written explanations live in the NARRATIVE_* constants below. The function
  5 | inventory, the test inventory, the Keycloak realm summary and the full-source
  6 | appendix are generated from the real files, so they cannot drift from the code.
  7 | 
  8 | Run from the project root:  python3 scripts/generate-code-reference.py
  9 | """
 10 | from __future__ import annotations
 11 | 
 12 | import ast
 13 | import json
 14 | import re
 15 | import subprocess
 16 | import textwrap
 17 | from datetime import date
 18 | from pathlib import Path
 19 | 
 20 | ROOT = Path(__file__).resolve().parent.parent
 21 | OUT = ROOT / "docs" / "FULL_CODE_REFERENCE.txt"
 22 | W = 100  # wrap width
 23 | 
 24 | 
 25 | def tracked_files() -> list[str]:
 26 |     out = subprocess.run(["git", "ls-files"], cwd=ROOT, capture_output=True, text=True, check=True)
 27 |     return sorted(out.stdout.splitlines())
 28 | 
 29 | 
 30 | def read(rel: str) -> str:
 31 |     return (ROOT / rel).read_text(encoding="utf-8", errors="replace")
 32 | 
 33 | 
 34 | def nlines(rel: str) -> int:
 35 |     return len(read(rel).splitlines())
 36 | 
 37 | 
 38 | def h1(title: str) -> str:
 39 |     bar = "=" * W
 40 |     return f"\n\n{bar}\n{title}\n{bar}\n"
 41 | 
 42 | 
 43 | def h2(title: str) -> str:
 44 |     return f"\n{title}\n{'-' * len(title)}\n"
 45 | 
 46 | 
 47 | def wrap(text: str, indent: str = "") -> str:
 48 |     paras = [p.strip() for p in text.strip().split("\n\n")]
 49 |     return "\n\n".join(
 50 |         textwrap.fill(" ".join(p.split()), width=W, initial_indent=indent, subsequent_indent=indent)
 51 |         for p in paras
 52 |     )
 53 | 
 54 | 
 55 | # --------------------------------------------------------------------------------------
 56 | # Hand-written narrative
 57 | # --------------------------------------------------------------------------------------
 58 | 
 59 | NARRATIVE_OVERVIEW = """
 60 | manage-system is an inventory management web application. Users sign in through Keycloak
 61 | (OpenID Connect), manage products, record stock movements (entries and exits), and managers
 62 | can read reports, an audit trail, and administer user accounts. The same business logic is
 63 | available in two ways: server-rendered web pages (Flask + Jinja2 + HTMX) and a JSON REST API
 64 | protected by JWT bearer tokens with an OpenAPI/Swagger description.
 65 | 
 66 | Around the application there is a complete quality and operations pipeline: automated tests
 67 | (unit, API contract, data/migration, end-to-end browser, load), three CI systems (GitHub
 68 | Actions, Jenkins, SonarCloud analysis), a staging stack with a security scan, and an
 69 | observability stack (Prometheus, Grafana, Loki, Tempo, Alloy, Alertmanager).
 70 | 
 71 | TECHNOLOGY STACK
 72 |   Language / framework   Python 3.12, Flask, Flask-Smorest (blueprints + OpenAPI), Marshmallow
 73 |   Database               PostgreSQL 16 via SQLAlchemy 2 / Flask-SQLAlchemy, Flask-Migrate (Alembic)
 74 |   Authentication         Keycloak 24 (realm "inventory-realm"), Authlib (browser login), PyJWT (API)
 75 |   Front end              Jinja2 templates, HTMX for partial page updates, no separate SPA
 76 |   Telemetry              OpenTelemetry (traces), prometheus_client (metrics), JSON logs
 77 |   Tests                  pytest, pytest-cov, Schemathesis, Playwright, k6
 78 |   Containers             Docker, Docker Compose
 79 |   CI / quality           GitHub Actions, Jenkins, SonarCloud, pip-audit, OWASP ZAP
 80 | """
 81 | 
 82 | NARRATIVE_ARCH = r"""
 83 |                          +---------------------------+
 84 |    Browser  ------------>|  Flask app  (app/main.py) |<------------ API clients (Bearer JWT)
 85 |    (HTML pages, HTMX)    |  port 5000                |
 86 |                          |  UI routes + /api/* APIs  |
 87 |                          +-----+-----------+---------+
 88 |                                |           |
 89 |                   SQLAlchemy   |           | OIDC login, JWKS, admin API
 90 |                                v           v
 91 |                         +-----------+   +--------------------+
 92 |                         | Postgres  |   | Keycloak 24        |
 93 |                         | port 5432 |   | port 8080          |
 94 |                         +-----------+   | realm inventory-   |
 95 |                                         | realm              |
 96 |                                         +--------------------+
 97 | 
 98 |    Telemetry from Flask:   /metrics (Prometheus scrape)  +  OTLP traces -> Alloy -> Tempo;  stdout logs -> Alloy -> Loki
 99 |    Dashboards and alerts:  Prometheus -> Alertmanager;  Grafana reads Prometheus, Loki, Tempo

101 | 
102 | NARRATIVE_FLOWS = """
103 | FLOW 1 - Browser login (OpenID Connect authorization code flow)
104 |   1. The user opens any protected page. The login_required decorator finds no "user" in the Flask
105 |      session and redirects to /auth/login-page, which shows a button.
106 |   2. The button goes to /auth/login. Authlib builds the callback URL (/auth/callback) and
107 |      redirects the browser to Keycloak's authorization endpoint.
108 |   3. The user types credentials at Keycloak. Keycloak redirects back to /auth/callback with a code.
109 |   4. callback() exchanges the code for tokens (authorize_access_token), reads the ID-token user
110 |      info, decodes the access token WITHOUT verifying the signature (it came straight from
111 |      Keycloak over the back channel and is only used to read role names) and keeps only the realm
112 |      roles that contain ":" (for example product:manage). These are stored in the session as
113 |      user_scopes. The user is redirected to "/".
114 |   5. From now on, require_ui_scope("x:y") checks user_scopes before sensitive pages. A missing
115 |      scope produces a flash message and a redirect to the home page.
116 |   6. /auth/logout clears the session and redirects to Keycloak's logout endpoint, which then
117 |      returns the browser to the app.
118 | 
119 | FLOW 2 - API call with a JWT
120 |   1. The client obtains a token from Keycloak (password grant, or through the admin UI).
121 |   2. It sends "Authorization: Bearer <token>". require_jwt downloads Keycloak's public keys once
122 |      (JWKS), caches them in a module variable, finds the key by the token's "kid" header, and
123 |      verifies the RS256 signature and expiry.
124 |   3. require_scope("product:view") then checks that the role exists in realm_access.roles.
125 |      Missing header/invalid token -> 401, expired -> 401, wrong role -> 403.
126 |   4. In tests (app.config["TESTING"] = True) both decorators are bypassed and a fake claim set
127 |      with every role is injected, so API tests do not need Keycloak.
128 | 
129 | FLOW 3 - Creating a product
130 |   UI: POST /products reads the HTML form, requires name, SKU and price, rejects a duplicate SKU,
131 |       converts numbers, inserts a Product, commits, bumps the products_created_total counter and
132 |       refreshes the products_total gauge, and flashes a success message.
133 |   API: POST /api/products validates the JSON with the same rules and returns 201 with the new
134 |       product, or 400 for a missing field or duplicate SKU.
135 |   Either way the SQLAlchemy "after_insert" listener writes an AuditLog row (see Flow 5).
136 | 
137 | FLOW 4 - Recording a stock movement
138 |   The movement type must be "entry" or "exit" and the quantity a positive integer. An exit is
139 |   rejected when it would take the quantity below zero. Otherwise the product quantity changes,
140 |   a StockMovement row records previous and new quantity, user and notes, and the transaction is
141 |   committed. stock_movements_total{type,product} is incremented. Changing a product's quantity
142 |   through the product edit form or PUT /api/products/<id> creates a movement automatically
143 |   (register_qty_change_movement).
144 | 
145 | FLOW 5 - Audit trail
146 |   audit/listeners.py registers SQLAlchemy listeners on Product and StockMovement for
147 |   after_insert, after_update and after_delete. Each listener builds an AuditLog row containing
148 |   the table, record id, action (INSERT/UPDATE/DELETE), old and new values as JSON, the acting user
149 |   (from the session or request context) and a timestamp. Rows for other tables are ignored, and
150 |   an update that changed nothing is skipped. The managers' /audit page and /api/audit read them.
151 | 
152 | FLOW 6 - User administration
153 |   Only users with user:manage reach /users and /api/users. The app never stores users itself:
154 |   keycloak_admin.py logs in to Keycloak as the master-realm admin (password grant against
155 |   admin-cli), then calls Keycloak's admin REST API to list, create, read users, list realm roles
156 |   and replace a user's application roles. After a role change it logs the user out of all
157 |   sessions so the new permissions apply at the next login.
158 | 
159 | ROLES AND PERMISSIONS (realm roles that the application understands)
160 |   group:employee, group:manager         broad groups
161 |   product:view, product:manage          read / write products
162 |   stock:view,   stock:manage            read / write stock movements
163 |   report:view                           reports page and /api/reports
164 |   audit:view                            audit page and /api/audit
165 |   user:manage                           user administration
166 |   alice_worker (employee) can see products and stock. kratos_boss (manager) additionally sees
167 |   reports, audit and users.
168 | """
169 |

229 | NARRATIVE_CI = """
230 | GITHUB ACTIONS - .github/workflows/ci.yml  (workflow name "CI")
231 |   Triggers: every push and every pull request to main or dev. A concurrency group cancels an older
232 |   run of the same branch when a new one starts. Shared environment: Python 3.12, a CI Postgres
233 |   user/password/database, DATABASE_URL, FLASK_APP and PYTHONPATH.
234 |   Jobs (a job lists "needs" when it waits for others):
235 |     build              checkout, set up Python with pip cache, install requirements, verify that
236 |                        "from app.main import app" imports.
237 |     unit-tests         (needs build) pytest on tests/test_products.py and tests/test_stock.py,
238 |                        uploads a report artifact.
239 |     api-tests          (needs build) Schemathesis contract tests (tests/test_contract.py), artifact.
240 |     integration-tests  (needs build) starts a Postgres service container, runs "flask db upgrade",
241 |                        then pytest on tests/data (migrations and the duplicate-SKU rule), artifact.
242 |     coverage           (needs unit, api, integration) Postgres service, migrations, whole non-E2E suite
243 |                        with --cov, uploads coverage.xml as an artifact.
244 |     e2e                (needs unit, api) brings up the CI compose stack (Postgres, Keycloak, Flask),
245 |                        waits for Keycloak and Flask, applies migrations, resets the Keycloak test
246 |                        user passwords, installs Playwright and runs the 4 browser tests, uploads the
247 |                        report, dumps logs on failure and always stops the stack.
248 |     security           (needs build) pip-audit against requirements.txt, uploads the audit.
249 |     docker-build       (needs unit, api, integration) builds the application image.

252 |   Manual only (workflow_dispatch). Starts a Postgres service, installs dependencies, runs

254 |   in coverage.xml start with "app/", which is what SonarCloud needs to match files), then runs

256 |   secrets. Project key, organization and exclusions come from sonar-project.properties.
257 |   NOTE: SonarCloud's free plan only shows the main branch, and Automatic Analysis must be OFF
258 |   in the SonarCloud project, otherwise the scan fails with "running CI analysis while Automatic
259 |   Analysis is enabled".

262 |   organization uziel-almonte, project key Uziel-Almonte_manage-system, sources = app, tests = tests,
263 |   Python 3.12, coverage from coverage.xml. Excluded from analysis: __pycache__, E2E tests, Alembic
264 |   migrations and the Keycloak realm export (it contains development signing keys). Excluded from
265 |   coverage only: scripts/, generate_openapi_spec.py, tests/ and migrations/.
266 | 
267 | JENKINS - Jenkinsfile (declarative pipeline, any agent)
268 |   Options: no concurrent builds, 60-minute timeout. Environment: image name manage-system with the
269 |   build number as tag, a per-build compose project name, CI database URL, paths to the docker and
270 |   docker-compose binaries inside the Jenkins container, and HOST_MOUNT (the host path to mount
271 |   into sibling containers, because Jenkins itself runs in Docker).
272 |   Stages, in order:
273 |     1. Checkout                     checkout scm.
274 |     2. Build                        docker build of the image, then a smoke import of app.main.
275 |     3. Prepare CI                   scripts/ci-free-host-ports.sh frees ports 5000/8080/5432.
276 |     4. Tests (parallel)             Unit tests; API / contract tests; Data / migration tests (starts
277 |                                     a CI Postgres, runs "flask db upgrade" and tests/data, always

280 |     6. Security                     pip-audit inside the built image.
281 |     7. E2E                          CI compose stack, waits, migrations, Keycloak user preparation,

284 |                                     docker-compose.yml stack, waits for services, migrates, prepares
285 |                                     Keycloak users, runs scripts/verify-stack.sh and a k6 smoke test.
286 |     9. Docker image                 tags the build as manage-system:ci.
287 |   Post (always): tears down every compose project it created so nothing is left behind.
288 |   Jenkins itself is defined by jenkins/Dockerfile (Jenkins LTS + Docker CLI + Compose + plugins),
289 |   jenkins/plugins.txt, jenkins/casc.yaml (Configuration as Code: users and the job "manage-system")
290 |   and jenkins/jenkins-entrypoint.sh. It is started with "docker compose --profile jenkins up -d
291 |   --build jenkins" on http://localhost:8090.

294 | NARRATIVE_INFRA = """
295 | DOCKERFILE (application image)
296 |   Starts from python:3.12-slim, sets /app as the working directory, copies requirements.txt first
297 |   (so the dependency layer is cached), installs requirements without a pip cache, copies the rest
298 |   of the project, exposes port 5000 and starts "flask --app app.main run --host=0.0.0.0 --port=5000".
299 | 
300 | docker-compose.yml  (local / demo stack)
301 |   web (flask_app :5000)        the app; depends on db and keycloak.
302 |   db (postgres_db :5432)       PostgreSQL 16 with the postgres_data volume.
303 |   keycloak (keycloak_auth :8080)  Keycloak 24 "start-dev --import-realm"; imports
304 |                                keycloak/realm-export.json on the first start; uses Postgres.
305 |   e2e                          Playwright container, profile "e2e" (not started by default).
306 |   zap                          OWASP ZAP baseline scan, profile "security".
307 |   pip-audit                    dependency audit container, profile "security".
308 |   prometheus :9090, postgres_exporter :9187, node-exporter :9100, tempo :3200, loki :3100,
309 |   grafana :3000, alertmanager :9093, alloy :4317/:4318/:12345   the observability stack.
310 |   jenkins (jenkins_ci :8090)   profile "jenkins".
311 |   Named volumes: postgres_data, prometheus_data, tempo_data, loki_data, grafana_data, alloy_data,
312 |   jenkins_home. "docker compose down" keeps them; "down -v" DELETES them (this is why users and
313 |   products disappear after "down -v" or after the full test script).
314 | 
315 | docker-compose.staging.yml   Same application from the BUILT image under Gunicorn, with health
316 |   checks and a separate Keycloak database (keycloak-db-init creates it), plus the observability
317 |   stack and a ZAP baseline scan. Run through scripts/run-staging-qa.sh.
318 | docker-compose.ci.yml        Small stack used by CI: db, keycloak-db-init, keycloak and web.
319 | docker-compose.sonar.yml     Local SonarQube (community) on :9000 with its own volumes.
320 | 
321 | OBSERVABILITY CONFIGURATION (observability/)
322 |   prometheus/prometheus.yml       scrape jobs: prometheus, tempo, loki, alloy, flask, node_exporter,
323 |                                   postgres_exporter. rules/alerts.rules.yml defines the alerts; rules/alerts.yml
324 |                                   only holds TargetDown (up == 0 for 1 min, team infra).
325 |   Alert rules                     HighCPUUsage (>85%), CriticalCPUUsage (>95%), HighMemoryUsage
326 |                                   (>90%), DBPoolNearExhaustion (>90% of pool), HighErrorRate (>5% 5xx),
327 |                                   ElevatedErrorRate (>1%), HighLatencyP95 (>1 s), HighAuthFailureRate,
328 |                                   SpikeInvalidTokens (>50 in 15 min), AuthFailuresFromSingleIP (>20
329 |                                   in 10 min), KeycloakFailedLogins (>5 failed Keycloak logins from one IP in 5 min),
330 |                                   TargetDown. HighErrorRate exists once only (the old duplicate
331 |                                   in alerts.yml was removed).
332 |   alertmanager/alertmanager.yml   routes alerts by severity and team to the receivers default,
333 |                                   infra-team, backend-team, security-team and the *-critical ones.
334 |   alloy/config.alloy              Grafana Alloy receives OTLP traces from Flask (ports 4317 gRPC, 4318
335 |                                   HTTP) and forwards them to Tempo. It also tails every container's
336 |                                   stdout through the Docker socket and pushes it to Loki, labelled
337 |                                   with `container` (flask_app, grafana...) and `service_name`
338 |                                   (compose service). Filter in Grafana with {container="flask_app"}.
339 |                                   Failed Keycloak logins (wrong password happens on Keycloak's page,
340 |                                   Flask never sees it): Keycloak logs type="LOGIN_ERROR"; Alloy's
341 |                                   loki.process counts those lines and exposes
342 |                                   loki_process_custom_keycloak_login_failures_total{source_ip,reason}
343 |                                   on :12345/metrics, which Prometheus scrapes (job alloy).
344 |   tempo/tempo.yaml, loki/loki.yaml   storage and server settings for traces and logs.
345 |   grafana/provisioning            datasources (Prometheus, Loki, Tempo) and four dashboards:
346 |                                   Application (latency, throughput, errors), Infrastructure (CPU,
347 |                                   memory, DB pool), Business (products and stock movements) and
348 |                                   Security (authentication failures, invalid tokens and failed Keycloak logins).
349 |                                   Panels use increase()/rate() windows and `or vector(0)` so they
350 |                                   show 0 instead of "No data" when nothing has failed. The Loki

447 | def leading_doc(rel: str, node: ast.AST, tree: ast.AST, src: str) -> str | None:
448 |     """Documentation written just above a def as a bare string literal or comment block."""
449 |     parent = None
450 |     for p in ast.walk(tree):
451 |         if node in getattr(p, "body", []):
452 |             parent = p
453 |             break
454 |     if parent is not None:
455 |         body = parent.body
456 |         i = body.index(node)
457 |         if i > 0:
458 |             prev = body[i - 1]
459 |             if (isinstance(prev, ast.Expr) and isinstance(getattr(prev, "value", None), ast.Constant)
460 |                     and isinstance(prev.value.value, str) and node.lineno - prev.end_lineno <= 2):
461 |                 return prev.value.value
462 |     lines = src.splitlines()
463 |     top = node.lineno - 1 - len(node.decorator_list)
464 |     block = []
465 |     j = top - 1
466 |     while j >= 0 and lines[j].lstrip().startswith("#"):
467 |         block.append(lines[j].lstrip("# ").rstrip())
468 |         j -= 1
469 |     return "\n".join(reversed(block)) if block else None
470 | 
471 | 
472 | def sig_of(node: ast.AST) -> str:
473 |     args = ast.unparse(node.args) if hasattr(node, "args") else ""
474 |     ret = f" -> {ast.unparse(node.returns)}" if getattr(node, "returns", None) else ""
475 |     prefix = "async def" if isinstance(node, ast.AsyncFunctionDef) else "def"
476 |     return f"{prefix} {node.name}({args}){ret}"
477 | 
478 | 
479 | def doc_block(doc: str | None, indent: str) -> str:
480 |     if not doc:
481 |         return f"{indent}(no docstring)\n"
482 |     lines = [ln.rstrip() for ln in textwrap.dedent(doc).strip().splitlines()]
483 |     return "".join(f"{indent}{ln}\n" if ln else "\n" for ln in lines)
484 | 
485 | 
486 | def walk_defs(tree: ast.AST):
487 |     """Yield (qualified_name, node, depth) for every function and class."""
488 |     def visit(node, prefix, depth):
489 |         for child in ast.iter_child_nodes(node):
490 |             if isinstance(child, (ast.FunctionDef, ast.AsyncFunctionDef, ast.ClassDef)):
491 |                 q = f"{prefix}{child.name}"
492 |                 yield q, child, depth
493 |                 yield from visit(child, q + ".", depth + 1)
494 |             else:
495 |                 yield from visit(child, prefix, depth)
496 |     yield from visit(tree, "", 0)
497 | 
498 | 
499 | def module_docstring_and_globals(tree: ast.Module, src: str) -> list[str]:
500 |     notes = []
501 |     for node in tree.body:
502 |         if isinstance(node, ast.Assign) and node.col_offset == 0:
503 |             seg = ast.get_source_segment(src, node) or ""
504 |             first = seg.splitlines()[0] if seg else ""
505 |             notes.append(f"  line {node.lineno}: {first[:90]}")
506 |     return notes
507 | 
508 | 
509 | def python_inventory(files: list[str]) -> str:
510 |     out = []
511 |     for rel in files:
512 |         src = read(rel)
513 |         total = len(src.splitlines())
514 |         try:
515 |             tree = ast.parse(src)
516 |         except SyntaxError as e:  # pragma: no cover
517 |             out.append(h2(f"{rel}  (could not parse: {e})"))
518 |             continue
519 |         out.append(h2(f"{rel}   ({total} lines)"))
520 |         purpose = MODULE_PURPOSE.get(rel)
521 |         if purpose:
522 |             out.append(wrap(purpose) + "\n")
523 |         defs = list(walk_defs(tree))
524 |         if not defs:
525 |             out.append("  (no functions or classes)\n")
526 |             continue
527 |         out.append(f"  Functions and classes: {len(defs)}\n")
528 |         for qname, node, depth in defs:
529 |             ind = "  " + "    " * depth
530 |             kind = "class" if isinstance(node, ast.ClassDef) else "function"
531 |             end = getattr(node, "end_lineno", node.lineno)
532 |             out.append(f"\n{ind}[{kind}] {qname}   (lines {node.lineno}-{end})\n")
533 |             if kind == "function":
534 |                 for dec in node.decorator_list:
535 |                     out.append(f"{ind}  @{ast.unparse(dec)}\n")
536 |                 out.append(f"{ind}  {sig_of(node)}\n")
537 |             elif node.bases:
538 |                 out.append(f"{ind}  class {node.name}({', '.join(ast.unparse(b) for b in node.bases)})\n")
539 |             doc = ast.get_docstring(node)
540 |             if doc:
541 |                 out.append(doc_block(doc, ind + "  "))
542 |             else:
543 |                 lead = leading_doc(rel, node, tree, src)
544 |                 note = FUNC_NOTES.get(f"{rel}:{qname}")
545 |                 if lead:
546 |                     out.append(f"{ind}  (documentation written above the function in the source:)\n")
547 |                     out.append(doc_block(lead, ind + "  "))
548 |                 if note:
549 |                     out.append(f"{ind}  Summary: {note}\n")
550 |                 if not lead and not note:
551 |                     out.append(f"{ind}  (no docstring or comment in the source; see its code in the appendix)\n")
552 |     return "".join(out)
553 | 
554 | 
555 | def test_inventory(files: list[str]) -> str:
556 |     out = []
557 |     total_tests = 0
558 |     for rel in files:
559 |         src = read(rel)
560 |         tree = ast.parse(src)
561 |         out.append(h2(f"{rel}   ({len(src.splitlines())} lines)"))
562 |         md = ast.get_docstring(tree)
563 |         if md:
564 |             out.append(wrap(md, "  ") + "\n")
565 |         count = 0
566 |         for q, node, depth in walk_defs(tree):
567 |             if isinstance(node, ast.ClassDef):
568 |                 out.append(f"\n  [class] {q}\n")
569 |                 continue
570 |             is_test = node.name.startswith("test_")
571 |             is_fixture = any("fixture" in ast.unparse(d) for d in node.decorator_list)
572 |             if not (is_test or is_fixture):
573 |                 continue
574 |             tag = "test" if is_test else "fixture"
575 |             count += is_test
576 |             decs = [ast.unparse(d) for d in node.decorator_list if not d.__class__.__name__ == "Name"]
577 |             doc = ast.get_docstring(node)
578 |             first = (doc or "").strip().splitlines()[0] if doc else ""
579 |             human = node.name.replace("test_", "", 1).replace("_", " ")
580 |             out.append(f"    [{tag}] {q}   (lines {node.lineno}-{getattr(node, 'end_lineno', node.lineno)})\n")
581 |             for d in decs:
582 |                 if d.startswith("pytest.mark") or d.startswith("patch"):
583 |                     out.append(f"        {'@' + d[:120]}\n")
584 |             out.append(f"        {first if first else 'Checks: ' + human}\n")
585 |         total_tests += count
586 |         out.append(f"\n  Tests in this file: {count}\n")
587 |     out.append(f"\nTOTAL test functions listed (parametrized cases count once): {total_tests}\n")
588 |     return "".join(out)

591 | def shell_inventory(files: list[str]) -> str:
592 |     out = []
593 |     for rel in files:
594 |         src = read(rel)
595 |         lines = src.splitlines()
596 |         out.append(h2(f"{rel}   ({len(lines)} lines)"))
597 |         header = []
598 |         for ln in lines[1:40]:
599 |             if ln.startswith("#"):
600 |                 header.append(ln.lstrip("# ").rstrip())
601 |             elif header:
602 |                 break
603 |         if header:
604 |             out.append("  Purpose (from the script's own header):\n")
605 |             out.extend(f"    {h}\n" for h in header)
606 |         funcs = [(i + 1, m.group(1)) for i, ln in enumerate(lines)
607 |                  if (m := re.match(r"^\s*(?:function\s+)?([A-Za-z_][A-Za-z0-9_]*)\s*\(\)\s*\{", ln))]
608 |         if funcs:
609 |             out.append("  Shell functions:\n")
610 |             out.extend(f"    line {n}: {name}()\n" for n, name in funcs)
611 |     return "".join(out)

614 | def migrations_inventory(files: list[str]) -> str:
615 |     out = []
616 |     for rel in files:
617 |         src = read(rel)
618 |         rev = re.search(r"^revision\s*=\s*['\"](\w+)", src, re.M)
619 |         down = re.search(r"^down_revision\s*=\s*(.+)$", src, re.M)
620 |         doc = ast.get_docstring(ast.parse(src)) or ""
621 |         ops = re.findall(r"op\.(create_table|drop_table|add_column|drop_column|create_foreign_key|"
622 |                          r"drop_constraint|create_index|drop_index|alter_column)\(\s*['\"]?([\w_]*)", src)
623 |         out.append(h2(rel))
624 |         out.append(f"  revision {rev.group(1) if rev else '?'}   down_revision {down.group(1).strip() if down else '?'}\n")
625 |         if doc:
626 |             out.append(f"  {doc.splitlines()[0]}\n")
627 |         if ops:
628 |             out.append("  operations: " + "; ".join(f"{a} {b}".strip() for a, b in ops) + "\n")
629 |     return "".join(out)

632 | def templates_inventory(files: list[str]) -> str:
633 |     out = []
634 |     for rel in files:
635 |         src = read(rel)
636 |         ext = re.search(r"{%\s*extends\s+['\"]([^'\"]+)", src)
637 |         blocks = re.findall(r"{%\s*block\s+(\w+)", src)
638 |         out.append(h2(f"{rel}   ({len(src.splitlines())} lines)"))
639 |         out.append(f"  {TEMPLATE_PURPOSE.get(rel, '')}\n")
640 |         if ext:
641 |             out.append(f"  extends: {ext.group(1)}\n")
642 |         if blocks:
643 |             out.append(f"  blocks: {', '.join(blocks)}\n")
644 |         ctx = sorted(set(re.findall(r"{{\s*([a-zA-Z_][\w]*)", src)) - {"url_for", "loop", "super"})
645 |         if ctx:
646 |             out.append(f"  variables used: {', '.join(ctx[:20])}\n")
647 |         hx = sorted(set(re.findall(r"hx-(?:get|post|put|delete|target|swap|trigger)", src)))
648 |         if hx:
649 |             out.append(f"  HTMX attributes: {', '.join(hx)}\n")
650 |     return "".join(out)

653 | def realm_summary() -> str:
654 |     realms = json.loads(read("keycloak/realm-export.json"))
655 |     if isinstance(realms, dict):
656 |         realms = [realms]
657 |     out = [f"  File: keycloak/realm-export.json ({nlines('keycloak/realm-export.json')} lines, NOT reproduced "
658 |            "in the appendix\n  because it contains development signing keys). Keycloak imports it on first "
659 |            "start. It holds\n  two realms: master (Keycloak's own admin realm) and inventory-realm (this app).\n"]
660 |     for data in realms:
661 |         out.append(f"\n  Realm: {data.get('realm')}   enabled: {data.get('enabled')}   "
662 |                    f"sslRequired: {data.get('sslRequired')}\n")
663 |         out.append("  Clients:\n")
664 |         for c in data.get("clients", []):
665 |             out.append(f"    - {c['clientId']:<24} public={c.get('publicClient')}  "
666 |                        f"standardFlow={c.get('standardFlowEnabled')}  "
667 |                        f"directGrants={c.get('directAccessGrantsEnabled')}\n")
668 |         roles = [r["name"] for r in data.get("roles", {}).get("realm", [])]
669 |         out.append("  Realm roles: " + ", ".join(roles) + "\n")
670 |         out.append("  Users in the export:\n")
671 |         for u in data.get("users", []):
672 |             out.append(f"    - {u['username']:<14} roles: {', '.join(u.get('realmRoles', []))}\n")
673 |     return "".join(out)

676 | APPENDIX_EXT = {".py", ".sh", ".yml", ".yaml", ".js", ".html", ".alloy", ".ini", ".properties", ".txt"}
677 | APPENDIX_NAMES = {"Dockerfile", "Jenkinsfile", ".env.example", ".gitignore", "TODO"}
678 | APPENDIX_SKIP_PREFIX = ("docs/", "reports/", "security-reports/", "htmlcov/", ".tools/", "tests/e2e/screenshots/")

682 | def appendix_files(all_files: list[str]) -> list[str]:
683 |     keep = []
684 |     for f in all_files:
685 |         if f.startswith(APPENDIX_SKIP_PREFIX) or f in APPENDIX_SKIP:
686 |             continue
687 |         p = Path(f)
688 |         if f == "keycloak/realm-export.json":
689 |             continue
690 |         if p.suffix in APPENDIX_EXT or p.name in APPENDIX_NAMES or p.name.startswith("Dockerfile") \
691 |                 or f.startswith("observability/") and p.suffix == ".json" or f == "openapi_spec.json":
692 |             keep.append(f)
693 |     return keep

695 | 
696 | def appendix(files: list[str]) -> str:
697 |     out = []
698 |     for rel in files:
699 |         text = read(rel)
700 |         lines = text.splitlines()
701 |         out.append(f"\n{'#' * W}\n# FILE: {rel}   ({len(lines)} lines)\n{'#' * W}\n")
702 |         width = len(str(len(lines)))
703 |         for i, ln in enumerate(lines, 1):
704 |             out.append(f"{i:>{width}} | {ln}\n")
705 |     return "".join(out)

708 | def main() -> None:
709 |     files = tracked_files()
710 |     py_app = [f for f in files if f.startswith("app/") and f.endswith(".py")]
711 |     py_tests = [f for f in files if f.startswith("tests/") and f.endswith(".py") and not f.endswith("__init__.py")]
712 |     py_scripts = [f for f in files if (f.startswith("scripts/") or f == "generate_openapi_spec.py")
713 |                   and f.endswith(".py")]
714 |     sh_scripts = [f for f in files if f.startswith("scripts/") and f.endswith(".sh")] + \
715 |                  [f for f in files if f == "jenkins/jenkins-entrypoint.sh"]
716 |     migrations = [f for f in files if f.startswith("migrations/versions/") and f.endswith(".py")]
717 |     templates = [f for f in files if f.startswith("app/templates/")]
718 |     k6 = [f for f in files if f.startswith("tests/k6/") and f.endswith(".js")]
719 |     app_loc = sum(nlines(f) for f in py_app)
720 |     test_loc = sum(nlines(f) for f in py_tests)
721 |     appx = appendix_files(files)
722 | 
723 |     out = []
724 |     out.append("=" * W + "\n")
725 |     out.append("MANAGE-SYSTEM - COMPLETE CODE REFERENCE\n")
726 |     out.append(f"Generated {date.today().isoformat()} by scripts/generate-code-reference.py\n")
727 |     out.append("=" * W + "\n")
728 |     out.append(wrap("""
729 | This single text file documents the whole project: every Python function and class (with its
730 | docstring, line range, decorators and signature), the tests, the CI/CD pipelines, Docker and
731 | observability configuration, and - in the appendix - the complete source of every code and
732 | configuration file with line numbers. Sections 1 to 3 and 7 to 9 are written by hand; the
733 | inventories are generated from the code. Function descriptions are quoted from the project's own
734 | docstrings, which are written in Spanish with six headings: "Qué hace" (what it does), "Por qué
735 | lo hace" (why), "Cómo lo hace" (how), "De dónde viene" (where the input comes from), "A dónde va"
736 | (where the output goes) and "Librerías externas" (external libraries used).

813 |   Never run "docker compose down -v" or scripts/run-full-test-suite.sh beforehand: both delete the
814 |   Keycloak users and the data.
815 |   1. docker compose up -d ; docker compose exec web flask db upgrade ; bash scripts/verify-stack.sh
816 |   2. If login fails: bash scripts/prepare-keycloak-e2e.sh (restores kratos_boss / alice_worker).
817 |   3. Sign in at http://localhost:5000 as kratos_boss / password123, create a product and make a few
818 |      stock movements, then repeat a minute later. Rate/increase panels need two Prometheus scrapes
819 |      (about 30 s), and counters restart from zero whenever the web container restarts.
820 |   4. Click around for a minute or two to build traffic.
821 |   5. Security demo: 30 requests with a bad token from one IP
822 |        for i in $(seq 1 30); do curl -s -o /dev/null -H "Authorization: Bearer bad" localhost:5000/api/products; done
823 |      This fills the Security dashboard and fires SpikeInvalidTokens and AuthFailuresFromSingleIP
824 |      (they route to the security receivers in Alertmanager and clear on their own).
825 |   Tabs: App :5000, Grafana :3000 (admin/admin, folder "Observability"), Prometheus :9090
826 |   (Status > Targets: 7 up), Alertmanager :9093, Keycloak :8080 (admin/admin).
827 |   Logs and traces: Grafana > Explore > Loki, query {container="flask_app"}, click "View Trace".
828 |   6. Failed-login demo: type a wrong password on the Keycloak login page 6+ times (or use the curl
829 |      below), wait ~1 minute: the Security dashboard shows the failed logins and KeycloakFailedLogins fires.
830 |        for i in $(seq 1 8); do curl -s -o /dev/null -d grant_type=password -d client_id=flask-backend -d username=kratos_boss -d password=WRONG http://localhost:8080/realms/inventory-realm/protocol/openid-connect/token; done
831 |   Empty panel? Wait about 30 s and send more traffic.
832 | """)

# FILE: scripts/generate-presentation-docs.py   (487 lines)

  1 | #!/usr/bin/env python3
  2 | """Generate presentation DOCX files for Uziel and Erick."""
  3 | from __future__ import annotations
  4 | 
  5 | from pathlib import Path
  6 | 
  7 | from docx import Document
  8 | from docx.enum.text import WD_BREAK
  9 | from docx.shared import Pt
 10 | from docx.enum.style import WD_STYLE_TYPE
 11 | 
 12 | ROOT = Path(__file__).resolve().parents[1]
 13 | OUT = ROOT / "docs" / "presentation"
 14 | 
 15 | 
 16 | def read_snippet(rel_path: str, start: int, end: int) -> str:
 17 |     lines = (ROOT / rel_path).read_text(encoding="utf-8").splitlines()
 18 |     return "\n".join(lines[start - 1 : end])
 19 | 
 20 | 
 21 | def setup_styles(doc: Document) -> None:
 22 |     normal = doc.styles["Normal"]
 23 |     normal.font.name = "Calibri"
 24 |     normal.font.size = Pt(11)
 25 |     if "CodeBlock" not in [s.name for s in doc.styles]:
 26 |         style = doc.styles.add_style("CodeBlock", WD_STYLE_TYPE.PARAGRAPH)
 27 |         style.font.name = "Consolas"
 28 |         style.font.size = Pt(9)
 29 | 
 30 | 
 31 | def add_title(doc: Document, title: str, subtitle: str) -> None:
 32 |     doc.add_heading(title, 0)
 33 |     p = doc.add_paragraph(subtitle)
 34 |     p.runs[0].italic = True
 35 |     doc.add_paragraph()
 36 | 
 37 | 
 38 | def add_h(doc: Document, text: str, level: int = 1) -> None:
 39 |     doc.add_heading(text, level=level)
 40 | 
 41 | 
 42 | def add_p(doc: Document, text: str) -> None:
 43 |     doc.add_paragraph(text)
 44 | 
 45 | 
 46 | def add_bullets(doc: Document, items: list[str]) -> None:
 47 |     for item in items:
 48 |         doc.add_paragraph(item, style="List Bullet")
 49 | 
 50 | 
 51 | def add_code(doc: Document, caption: str, code: str) -> None:
 52 |     add_p(doc, caption)
 53 |     for line in code.splitlines():
 54 |         p = doc.add_paragraph(line)
 55 |         if "CodeBlock" in [s.name for s in doc.styles]:
 56 |             p.style = doc.styles["CodeBlock"]
 57 |         for run in p.runs:
 58 |             run.font.name = "Consolas"
 59 |             run.font.size = Pt(8)
 60 |     doc.add_paragraph()
 61 | 
 62 | 
 63 | def add_page_break(doc: Document) -> None:
 64 |     doc.add_paragraph().add_run().add_break(WD_BREAK.PAGE)
 65 | 
 66 |

####################################################################################################
# FILE: scripts/prepare-keycloak-e2e.sh   (73 lines)
####################################################################################################
 1 | #!/usr/bin/env bash
 2 | # Prepare Keycloak test users for E2E and k6 (password + kratos profile).
 3 | set -euo pipefail
 4 | 
 5 | COMPOSE_FILE="${COMPOSE_FILE:-docker-compose.ci.yml}"
 6 | COMPOSE_PROJECT="${COMPOSE_PROJECT:-}"
 7 | 
 8 | # CI compose hard-codes admin/admin; don't let a leftover .env override that.
 9 | if [[ "$COMPOSE_FILE" == *docker-compose.ci.yml ]]; then
10 |   KC_ADMIN=admin
11 |   KC_ADMIN_PASSWORD=admin
12 | elif [[ -f .env ]]; then
13 |   set -a
14 |   # shellcheck disable=SC1091
15 |   source .env
16 |   set +a
17 |   KC_ADMIN="${KEYCLOAK_ADMIN:-${KEYCLOAK_USER:-admin}}"
18 |   KC_ADMIN_PASSWORD="${KEYCLOAK_ADMIN_PASSWORD:-${KEYCLOAK_PASSWORD:-admin}}"
19 | else
20 |   KC_ADMIN="${KEYCLOAK_ADMIN:-${KEYCLOAK_USER:-admin}}"
21 |   KC_ADMIN_PASSWORD="${KEYCLOAK_ADMIN_PASSWORD:-${KEYCLOAK_PASSWORD:-admin}}"
22 | fi
23 | 
24 | if [[ -n "${DOCKER_COMPOSE:-}" ]]; then
25 |   compose() {
26 |     if [[ -n "$COMPOSE_PROJECT" ]]; then
27 |       "$DOCKER_COMPOSE" -f "$COMPOSE_FILE" -p "$COMPOSE_PROJECT" "$@"
28 |     else
29 |       "$DOCKER_COMPOSE" -f "$COMPOSE_FILE" "$@"
30 |     fi
31 |   }
32 | elif command -v docker-compose >/dev/null 2>&1; then
33 |   compose() {
34 |     if [[ -n "$COMPOSE_PROJECT" ]]; then
35 |       docker-compose -f "$COMPOSE_FILE" -p "$COMPOSE_PROJECT" "$@"
36 |     else
37 |       docker-compose -f "$COMPOSE_FILE" "$@"
38 |     fi
39 |   }
40 | else
41 |   compose() {
42 |     if [[ -n "$COMPOSE_PROJECT" ]]; then
43 |       docker compose -f "$COMPOSE_FILE" -p "$COMPOSE_PROJECT" "$@"
44 |     else
45 |       docker compose -f "$COMPOSE_FILE" "$@"
46 |     fi
47 |   }
48 | fi
49 | 
50 | echo "==> Preparing Keycloak users via kcadm"
51 | compose exec -T \
52 |   -e KC_ADMIN="$KC_ADMIN" \
53 |   -e KC_ADMIN_PASSWORD="$KC_ADMIN_PASSWORD" \
54 |   keycloak bash -ec '
55 |   /opt/keycloak/bin/kcadm.sh config credentials \
56 |     --server http://localhost:8080 \
57 |     --realm master \
58 |     --user "$KC_ADMIN" \
59 |     --password "$KC_ADMIN_PASSWORD"
60 |   KRATOS_ID=$(/opt/keycloak/bin/kcadm.sh get users -r inventory-realm -q username=kratos_boss --fields id --format csv --noquotes | tail -1)
61 |   /opt/keycloak/bin/kcadm.sh update users/$KRATOS_ID -r inventory-realm \
62 |     -s firstName=Kratos \
63 |     -s lastName=Boss \
64 |     -s email=kratos@company.com \
65 |     -s emailVerified=true
66 |   for user in alice_worker kratos_boss; do
67 |     /opt/keycloak/bin/kcadm.sh set-password \
68 |       -r inventory-realm \
69 |       --username "$user" \
70 |       --new-password password123
71 |   done
72 | '
73 | echo "==> Keycloak users ready"

# FILE: scripts/run-full-test-suite.sh   (149 lines)
####################################################################################################
  1 | #!/usr/bin/env bash
  2 | # Run the complete test matrix locally (matches Jenkins / GitHub Actions).
  3 | set -euo pipefail
  4 | 
  5 | ROOT_DIR="$(cd "$(dirname "${BASH_SOURCE[0]}")/.." && pwd)"
  6 | cd "$ROOT_DIR"
  7 | 
  8 | export PYTHONPATH="$ROOT_DIR"
  9 | export FLASK_APP=app.main
 10 | export DATABASE_URL="${DATABASE_URL:-postgresql://postgres:ci_password@db:5432/inventory_db}"
 11 | COMPOSE_CI="${COMPOSE_CI:-docker-compose.ci.yml}"
 12 | COMPOSE_FULL="${COMPOSE_FULL:-docker-compose.yml}"
 13 | PROJECT_CI="${PROJECT_CI:-manage-system-test-ci}"
 14 | RUN_E2E="${RUN_E2E:-true}"
 15 | RUN_STACK="${RUN_STACK:-true}"
 16 | RUN_K6="${RUN_K6:-true}"
 17 | 
 18 | compose_ci() {
 19 |   docker compose -f "$COMPOSE_CI" -p "$PROJECT_CI" "$@"
 20 | }
 21 | 
 22 | compose_full() {
 23 |   docker compose -f "$COMPOSE_FULL" "$@"
 24 | }
 25 | 
 26 | cleanup() {
 27 |   compose_ci down -v --remove-orphans 2>/dev/null || true
 28 |   compose_full down -v --remove-orphans 2>/dev/null || true
 29 | }
 30 | 
 31 | write_report_index() {
 32 |   mkdir -p reports
 33 |   {
 34 |     echo "<!DOCTYPE html><html><head><meta charset=\"utf-8\"><title>Test reports</title></head><body>"
 35 |     echo "<h1>Test reports</h1><ul>"
 36 |     find reports security-reports -type f ! -name index.html 2>/dev/null | sort | while read -r file; do
 37 |       echo "<li><a href=\"../${file}\">${file}</a></li>"
 38 |     done
 39 |     echo "</ul></body></html>"
 40 |   } > reports/index.html
 41 | }
 42 | 
 43 | on_exit() {
 44 |   write_report_index || true
 45 |   cleanup
 46 | }
 47 | trap on_exit EXIT
 48 | 
 49 | mkdir -p reports
 50 | 
 51 | compose_network() {
 52 |   compose_ci ps -q db | head -1 | xargs -r docker inspect -f '{{range $k, $v := .NetworkSettings.Networks}}{{$k}}{{end}}'
 53 | }
 54 | 
 55 | python_runner() {
 56 |   docker run --rm \
 57 |     -v "$ROOT_DIR:/app" -w /app \
 58 |     -e PYTHONPATH=/app -e FLASK_APP=app.main -e DATABASE_URL="$DATABASE_URL" \
 59 |     --network host \
 60 |     python:3.12-slim \
 61 |     bash -lc "pip install -q -r requirements.txt && python3 $(printf '%q ' "$@")"
 62 | }
 63 | 
 64 | db_python_runner() {
 65 |   local net
 66 |   net="$(compose_network)"
 67 |   docker run --rm \
 68 |     -v "$ROOT_DIR:/app" -w /app \
 69 |     -e PYTHONPATH=/app -e FLASK_APP=app.main \
 70 |     -e DATABASE_URL=postgresql://postgres:ci_password@db:5432/inventory_db \
 71 |     --network "$net" \
 72 |     python:3.12-slim \
 73 |     bash -lc "pip install -q -r requirements.txt && python3 $(printf '%q ' "$@")"
 74 | }
 75 | 
 76 | echo "========== 1/8 Build =========="
 77 | docker build -t manage-system:local .
 78 | 
 79 | echo "========== 2/8 Unit tests =========="
 80 | python_runner -m pytest tests/test_products.py tests/test_stock.py -v --tb=short --junitxml=reports/junit-unit.xml
 81 | 
 82 | echo "========== 3/8 API / contract tests =========="
 83 | python_runner -m pytest tests/test_contract.py -v --tb=short --junitxml=reports/junit-api.xml
 84 | 
 85 | echo "========== 4/8 Data / migration tests =========="
 86 | compose_ci up -d db
 87 | until compose_ci exec -T db pg_isready -U postgres -d inventory_db >/dev/null 2>&1; do sleep 1; done
 88 | db_python_runner -m flask db upgrade
 89 | db_python_runner -m pytest tests/data/ -v --tb=short --junitxml=reports/junit-data.xml
 90 | compose_ci down -v
 91 | 
 92 | echo "========== 5/8 Coverage (non-E2E) =========="
 93 | compose_ci up -d db
 94 | until compose_ci exec -T db pg_isready -U postgres -d inventory_db >/dev/null 2>&1; do sleep 1; done
 95 | db_python_runner -m flask db upgrade
 96 | db_python_runner -m pytest tests/ -m "not e2e" --cov=app --cov-report=term-missing --cov-report=xml:reports/coverage.xml --cov-report=html:reports/htmlcov --junitxml=reports/junit-non-e2e.xml --tb=short
 97 | compose_ci down -v
 98 | 
 99 | echo "========== 6/8 Security (pip-audit) =========="
100 | docker run --rm -v "$ROOT_DIR:/app" -w /app python:3.12-slim \
101 |   bash -lc "pip install -q pip-audit && mkdir -p reports && pip-audit -r requirements.txt --format json -o reports/pip-audit.json && pip-audit -r requirements.txt"
102 | 
103 | if [[ "$RUN_E2E" == "true" ]]; then
104 |   echo "========== 7/8 E2E tests =========="
105 |   bash scripts/ci-free-host-ports.sh
106 |   compose_ci up -d --build
107 |   bash scripts/wait-for-services.sh \
108 |     "Flask" "http://localhost:5000/auth/login-page" \
109 |     "Keycloak" "http://localhost:8080/realms/inventory-realm/.well-known/openid-configuration"
110 |   compose_ci exec -T web flask db upgrade
111 |   COMPOSE_FILE="$COMPOSE_CI" COMPOSE_PROJECT="$PROJECT_CI" bash scripts/prepare-keycloak-e2e.sh
112 | 
113 |   if python3 -c "import playwright" 2>/dev/null; then
114 |     python3 -m pytest tests/e2e -m e2e -v --tb=short --junitxml=reports/junit-e2e.xml
115 |   else
116 |     docker run --rm --network host \
117 |       -v "$ROOT_DIR:/app" -w /app \
118 |       -e PYTHONPATH=/app \
119 |       -e E2E_BASE_URL=http://localhost:5000 \
120 |       mcr.microsoft.com/playwright/python:v1.60.0-jammy \
121 |       bash -lc "pip install -q -r requirements.txt && pytest tests/e2e -m e2e -v --tb=short --junitxml=reports/junit-e2e.xml"
122 |   fi
123 |   compose_ci down -v
124 | fi
125 | 
126 | if [[ "$RUN_STACK" == "true" ]]; then
127 |   echo "========== 8/8 Full stack + observability + k6 smoke =========="
128 |   compose_full down -v --remove-orphans 2>/dev/null || true
129 |   compose_full up -d --build
130 |   bash scripts/wait-for-services.sh
131 |   compose_full exec -T web flask db upgrade
132 |   COMPOSE_FILE="$COMPOSE_FULL" bash scripts/prepare-keycloak-e2e.sh
133 |   bash scripts/verify-stack.sh
134 | 
135 |   if [[ "$RUN_K6" == "true" ]]; then
136 |     mkdir -p reports
137 |     chmod 777 reports
138 |     docker run --rm --network host --user 0 \
139 |       -v "$ROOT_DIR:/app" -w /app \
140 |       -e BASE_URL=http://localhost:5000 \
141 |       -e KEYCLOAK_URL=http://localhost:8080 \
142 |       grafana/k6:0.53.0 run --summary-export=/app/reports/k6-smoke-summary.json tests/k6/smoke-test.js
143 |     test -s reports/k6-smoke-summary.json
144 |   fi
145 | fi
146 | 
147 | echo ""
148 | echo "Full test suite completed successfully."
149 | echo "Reports: reports/index.html reports/coverage.xml reports/htmlcov/"

 1 | #!/usr/bin/env bash
 2 | # Run Day 32 security checks: OWASP ZAP baseline + pip-audit.

 6 | ROOT_DIR="$(cd "$(dirname "${BASH_SOURCE[0]}")/.." && pwd)"

10 |

12 | docker compose up -d web db keycloak
13 | 
14 | echo "==> Waiting for Flask app..."
15 | for _ in $(seq 1 30); do

17 |     break
18 |   fi
19 |   sleep 2
20 | done

23 | docker compose --profile security run --rm zap

26 | docker compose --profile security run --rm pip-audit
27 |

# FILE: scripts/run-sonar-scan.sh   (35 lines)
####################################################################################################
 1 | #!/usr/bin/env bash
 2 | # Generate coverage and scan the project with a local SonarQube.
 3 | # Needs SONAR_TOKEN (create it in the SonarQube UI: My Account > Security).
 4 | # Does not touch the app stack or its volumes.
 5 | set -euo pipefail
 6 | 
 7 | ROOT_DIR="$(cd "$(dirname "${BASH_SOURCE[0]}")/.." && pwd)"
 8 | cd "$ROOT_DIR"
 9 | 
10 | SONAR_HOST_URL="${SONAR_HOST_URL:-http://localhost:9000}"
11 | : "${SONAR_TOKEN:?Set SONAR_TOKEN first (export SONAR_TOKEN=...)}"
12 | 
13 | if ! curl -fs "$SONAR_HOST_URL/api/system/status" | grep -q '"status":"UP"'; then
14 |   echo "SonarQube is not up at $SONAR_HOST_URL. Start it with:" >&2
15 |   echo "  docker compose -f docker-compose.sonar.yml -p manage-system-sonar up -d" >&2
16 |   exit 1
17 | fi
18 | 
19 | if [[ "${SKIP_COVERAGE:-false}" != "true" ]]; then
20 |   echo "==> Coverage (non-E2E, SQLite/unit + contract tests)"
21 |   docker run --rm -v "$ROOT_DIR:/app" -w /app \
22 |     -e PYTHONPATH=/app -e FLASK_APP=app.main \
23 |     python:3.12-slim \
24 |     bash -lc "pip install -q -r requirements.txt && pytest tests/test_products.py tests/test_stock.py tests/test_contract.py --cov=app --cov-report=xml:coverage.xml -q" \
25 |     || echo "WARN: coverage step failed, scanning without fresh coverage"
26 | fi
27 | 
28 | echo "==> SonarQube scan"
29 | docker run --rm --network host \
30 |   -v "$ROOT_DIR:/usr/src" \
31 |   -e SONAR_HOST_URL="$SONAR_HOST_URL" \
32 |   -e SONAR_TOKEN="$SONAR_TOKEN" \
33 |   sonarsource/sonar-scanner-cli
34 | 
35 | echo "Done. Results: $SONAR_HOST_URL/dashboard?id=manage-system"
